### Uncertain fields

## Vercel deploy model — Git integration, preview/production, environment variables and sensitive env, deployment protection, rollbacks, monorepos

### identity

- **name**: Vercel deploy model — Git integration, preview/production, environment variables and sensitive env, deployment protection, rollbacks, monorepos
- **item_id**: vercel-deploy-env-preview
- **cluster**: vercel
- **short_definition**: Vercel's deploy model connects a Git repository (GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure DevOps) so that pushes to the production branch create Production deployments and every other push, PR, or `vercel` CLI invocation creates a Preview deployment with a unique URL. Environments (Production, Preview, Development, plus Pro/Enterprise Custom Environments) each carry their own scoped environment variables, which can be marked Sensitive (write-only, encrypted, never re-readable). Deployment Protection (Vercel Authentication, Password Protection, Trusted IPs, Passport) gates who can view preview and/or production URLs, with a Protection Bypass secret for CI/E2E automation. Instant Rollback re-aliases domains to a prior production deployment without a rebuild. Monorepos are supported via per-directory Root Directory projects, automatic unaffected-build skipping, Ignored Build Step, and Turborepo remote caching. In this stack, Vercel also issues short-lived OIDC tokens (`VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN`) that AWS/GCP/Azure can federate against via `AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` / Workload Identity Federation, eliminating long-lived cloud keys in Vercel env vars.
- **scope_in**: Git provider integration and production-branch semantics; Preview vs Production vs Development vs Custom Environments; environment variable creation, scoping, size/count limits, and Sensitive env vars; Deployment Protection methods and bypass; Instant Rollback and manual promote/staging; monorepo project structure (Root Directory, Ignored Build Step, unaffected-project skipping, Related Projects); OIDC federation from Vercel to AWS/GCP for keyless backend access; `vercel.json`/CLI/REST API surfaces for the above.
- **scope_out**: Vercel Functions runtime internals and cold starts (see a functions/runtime item); Vercel Edge Network/CDN caching internals; Next.js framework-level rendering (ISR/PPR) (see a Next.js rendering item); Supabase branching/migrations mechanics (see the Supabase item — this item only covers how Vercel env vars point at Supabase per environment); Vercel Firewall/WAF and bot management rules beyond how they interact with Protection Bypass; billing/usage-based pricing beyond limits directly relevant to deploy/env/protection.
- **priority**: P0

### practice

**defaults** _Opinionated greenfield defaults for 2026 (what to pick without deliberation)_:
- Connect the repo via the native GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket integration (not a generic CI push) so PR comments, branch URLs, and fork-authorization checks work automatically.
- Keep `main` as the production branch; treat every other branch and every PR as a Preview deployment — this is the default and requires no configuration.
- Store all secrets as Vercel project Environment Variables scoped per environment (Production / Preview / Development), never hard-coded, never in `vercel.json`.
- Mark every credential-shaped Production and Preview env var (API keys, DB URLs with passwords, webhook signing secrets) as Sensitive; Sensitive is unavailable for Development, so keep local dev on separate low-privilege values pulled via `vercel env pull`.
- Prefer OIDC federation (`VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` → AWS `AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` / GCP Workload Identity Federation) over static `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` or a GCP service-account credential file stored as env vars, for any AWS/GCP resource Vercel Functions must reach.
- Enable Standard Protection with Vercel Authentication on all non-Hobby projects by default; add Protection Bypass secrets per automation consumer (CI, Playwright, uptime monitor) rather than disabling protection.
- Use a `staging` Custom Environment (Pro/Enterprise) or a dedicated long-lived preview branch with its own domain and Supabase branch for pre-prod QA that needs a stable URL, rather than relying on ephemeral per-PR URLs.
- For monorepos, create one Vercel Project per deployable app/service with Root Directory set, rely on automatic unaffected-project build skipping (workspaces-based), and only reach for a custom Ignored Build Step when the workspace-detection requirements aren't met (e.g. non-JS services).
- Treat Instant Rollback as the first response to a bad production deploy, and forward-fix + redeploy afterward; explicitly `vercel promote` to re-enable auto-assignment once resolved.
- Pin Node.js to the latest LTS actually supported by the project's `engines`/Vercel build image; verify current default/supported runtimes in Vercel project settings before assuming a specific version — do not hardcode a Node version claim from memory.
**decision_gates** _When to use / when not / which alternative (incl. the Supabase, Vercel, and VPS alternatives)_:
- Use Vercel's native Git integration when the team wants automatic PR previews, rollback, and deployment protection with minimal glue code — this is the default for a Next.js + Vercel stack.
- Use `vercel deploy` from a third-party CI (e.g. GitHub Actions) instead of/in addition to the native integration only when you need custom pre-deploy steps (schema migration gates, extra test matrices) that must block the deployment itself; combine with `--prebuilt` to avoid double builds.
- Use Custom Environments (Pro: 1, Enterprise: 12 per project) when you need a durable non-production environment with its own domain and env vars (staging/QA) that outlives a single PR; use a plain preview branch + branch-scoped env vars when you only need a temporary shared URL.
- Use Sensitive environment variables for anything that must not be readable after creation (service-role keys, signing secrets); use regular (non-sensitive) env vars for values that legitimately need to be re-read/copied by teammates (public config, non-secret feature flags) — Sensitive can only target Production/Preview, not Development.
- Use Standard Protection (protects everything except the current production domain) as the default; use All Deployments protection only when even production must stay behind auth (internal tools, not customer-facing); use Trusted IPs to protect only production while leaving preview URLs public — Enterprise only.
- Use Instant Rollback when the previous deployment (already aliased to production) is known-good and no destructive migration has run since; use a forward-fix + full redeploy when a database migration was applied, since rollback does not undo migrations or roll back env var changes.
- Use OIDC federation to AWS/GCP for any Vercel Function needing native cloud resource access (S3, RDS, GCS, BigQuery); fall back to a vendor SDK with its own token exchange (e.g. Supabase client keys) when the target isn't AWS/GCP/Azure and doesn't support OIDC.
- Use one Vercel Project per app in a monorepo (Root Directory setting) rather than a single project with a custom build script that builds multiple apps, so previews, env vars, and protection scope correctly per app.
**do_dont** _Concrete do and don't lists_:

- Do scope env vars per environment (Production/Preview/Development) explicitly rather than applying one var to all environments by default.
- Do mark credential-shaped Preview and Production env vars as Sensitive so they're encrypted and unreadable after creation.
- Do not reuse Production Stripe/Supabase/service-role keys in Preview — use test-mode keys and an isolated Supabase branch or staging project.
- Do use OIDC federation (`VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN`) for AWS/GCP access from Vercel Functions instead of storing long-lived cloud credential material as env vars.
- Do not assume `vercel env pull` output is safe to commit — `.env.local` must stay gitignored even though it holds only Development-scoped values.
- Do enable Deployment Protection (at least Standard Protection with Vercel Authentication) on any project that isn't intentionally fully public.
- Do not disable Deployment Protection just to make CI/E2E tests pass — use a Protection Bypass secret (`VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET`) instead.
- Do verify whether a pending database migration has run before using Instant Rollback — rollback reverts code and domain aliasing only, not schema state or env var values changed since that deployment.
- Do not treat `AWS_REGION` as stable when using OIDC-based AWS SDK calls from multi-region Vercel Functions — pin it explicitly as a project env var.
- Do set a unique Root Directory per Vercel Project in a monorepo rather than building multiple apps out of one project's build command.
- Do use branch-specific Preview env vars (CLI ≥22.0.0) to override values for a specific long-lived preview/staging branch instead of duplicating all Preview vars.
- Do not put anything larger than 64 KB total (all vars combined, per deployment) into environment variables; use external secret/config storage for larger blobs.
**config_skeleton** _Minimal correct config or IaC skeleton with placeholder identifiers only (English comments ok)_:
```json filename="vercel.json"
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "framework": "nextjs",
  "buildCommand": "pnpm build",
  "installCommand": "pnpm install --frozen-lockfile",
  "ignoreCommand": "npx turbo-ignore",
  "relatedProjects": ["prj_example"],
  "github": {
    "silent": false
  }
}
```

```ts filename="next.config.ts"
// Minimal Next.js 16 config relevant to Vercel deploys.
import type { NextConfig } from 'next';

const nextConfig: NextConfig = {
  // Framework env vars like NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_URL are injected by Vercel automatically;
  // do not hardcode the deployment host here.
  output: undefined, // default Vercel-managed build output; do not set 'export' if using Functions/ISR
};

export default nextConfig;
```

```ts filename="app/api/aws-example/route.ts"
// OIDC federation: exchange VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN for short-lived AWS credentials, no static keys.
import * as S3 from '@aws-sdk/client-s3';
import { awsCredentialsProvider } from '@vercel/oidc-aws-credentials-provider';

const AWS_REGION = process.env.AWS_REGION!; // pin explicitly, do not rely on runtime default
const AWS_ROLE_ARN = process.env.AWS_ROLE_ARN!; // e.g. arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/example-role

const s3client = new S3.S3Client({
  region: AWS_REGION,
  credentials: awsCredentialsProvider({
    audience: 'sts.amazonaws.com',
    roleArn: AWS_ROLE_ARN,
  }),
});

export async function GET() {
  const result = await s3client.send(
    new S3.ListObjectsV2Command({ Bucket: process.env.S3_BUCKET_NAME! }),
  );
  return Response.json(result.Contents?.map((o) => o.Key) ?? []);
}
```

```text filename="AWS trust-policy (placeholder team/account) — JSON structure"
Version: 2012-10-17
Statement:
  - Effect: Allow
    Principal:
      Federated: arn:aws:iam::123456789012:oidc-provider/oidc.vercel.com/team_example
    Action: sts:AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity
    Condition:
      StringEquals:
        oidc.vercel.com/team_example:aud: https://vercel.com/team_example
      StringLike:
        oidc.vercel.com/team_example:sub:
          - owner:team_example:project:*:environment:preview
          - owner:team_example:project:*:environment:production
```

```yaml filename=".github/workflows/preview-e2e.yml (excerpt)"
# Run Playwright against a protected Vercel Preview using Protection Bypass for Automation.
- name: Run E2E against Vercel Preview
  env:
    VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET: ${{ secrets.VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET }}
    PREVIEW_URL: https://my-app-git-feature-branch-team_example.vercel.app
  run: |
    pnpm playwright test --config=playwright.config.ts
```

```ts filename="playwright.config.ts (excerpt)"
import { defineConfig } from '@playwright/test';

if (!process.env.VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET) {
  throw new Error('VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET is required to test protected deployments');
}

export default defineConfig({
  use: {
    extraHTTPHeaders: {
      'x-vercel-protection-bypass': process.env.VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET,
      'x-vercel-set-bypass-cookie': 'true',
    },
  },
});
```
**failure_modes** _Top production failure modes and outage classes_:
- Instant rollback is used after a destructive migration ran; the app rolls back but the database schema does not, causing runtime errors against the reverted code (rollback does not touch DB state, only domain aliasing).
- A team disables the auto-assignment of production domains during an Instant Rollback (this happens automatically) and forgets to `vercel promote`/`Undo Rollback`, so subsequent pushes to `main` silently stop reaching production.
- Preview deployments inherit or are accidentally configured with Production Supabase/Stripe keys, exposing live data or charging real cards from a PR preview visible to anyone with the link.
- A Sensitive environment variable is attempted for the Development environment — blocked by the UI/API — leading to a fallback of storing the same secret as a plain (non-sensitive, readable) var, defeating the purpose.
- Standard Protection is enabled and `VERCEL_URL`/`VERCEL_BRANCH_URL`-based server-to-server fetches start failing with 401s because those generated URLs are no longer publicly reachable, and the code wasn't updated to use relative paths or forwarded cookies.
- A monorepo project's workspace `package.json` `name` fields collide or a dependency isn't declared, so Vercel's automatic unaffected-project build skipping misclassifies a changed package as unrelated and silently skips a build that should have run.
- OIDC trust policy `sub` condition is too permissive (e.g. matches any project or any environment) or too strict (exact match breaks when project name changes), causing either an overly broad blast radius or Function-time `AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` failures after a rename.
- `AWS_REGION` is treated as a fixed value by application code but Vercel Functions run multi-region, causing OIDC-authenticated AWS SDK calls to silently target a region where the resource doesn't exist.
- CI/E2E tests hardcode the Protection Bypass secret in a committed config file or client-visible query string logged by a third party, leaking a token that bypasses Vercel Authentication, Password Protection, and Trusted IPs for the whole project until rotated.
- A build depends on total environment variable payload exceeding 64 KB (large certs/JWTs plus other vars), causing an opaque build/runtime failure rather than a clear "env too large" message, especially on Edge/Middleware where the per-variable cap is only 5 KB.
**anti_patterns** _Patterns agents must not generate (static keys in CI, public buckets, open security groups, etc.)_:

- Storing static AWS/GCP long-lived credential material as Vercel environment variables instead of OIDC federation.
- Sharing one Supabase service-role key across Production and Preview environments.
- Disabling Deployment Protection to unblock a CI pipeline instead of issuing a Protection Bypass secret.
- Hardcoding a Protection Bypass secret or webhook `x-vercel-protection-bypass` query value in a committed file.
- Building multiple monorepo apps out of a single Vercel Project's custom build script instead of one Project per app with Root Directory set.
- Relying on Instant Rollback as a substitute for a tested forward-fix when a schema migration has already been applied.
- Treating `VERCEL_URL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_URL` as a stable public origin after enabling Standard/All Deployments protection.
- Putting large certificates or JWTs into env vars without checking the 64 KB total / 5 KB Edge-Function-per-variable caps.
- Granting an OIDC AWS trust policy a wildcard `sub` across all environments (dev/preview/production) instead of scoping per environment.

### tooling

**tools** _Primary CLIs/SDKs/consoles and alternatives_:

- Vercel CLI (`vercel`, `vercel env`, `vercel link`, `vercel promote`, `vercel deploy --prebuilt`)
- Vercel Dashboard (Deployments, Environments, Environment Variables, Deployment Protection settings)
- Vercel REST API / `@vercel/sdk` TypeScript SDK for programmatic env var and environment management
- @vercel/oidc-aws-credentials-provider (npm) for AWS SDK v3 credential exchange
- AWS IAM OIDC Identity Provider + STS AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity (federation target)
- GCP Workload Identity Federation (federation target, see /docs/oidc/gcp)
- Turborepo (`turbo-ignore`, `turbo query`) for monorepo build filtering and remote caching
- GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure DevOps native Git integration (PR comments, fork-authorization gate)
- Playwright (or other E2E runner) configured with Protection Bypass headers for preview testing
**versions_as_of** _Version and runtime pins as of research date (labeled as_of)_:
- Vercel CLI: use 22.0.0+ for Preview branch-scoped env vars, 21.0.1+ for Development env var CLI features, 20.1.0+ for `vercel link --repo` monorepo linking. [as_of 2026-08-29, source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables and https://vercel.com/docs/monorepos]
- OIDC Federation: generally available (per Vercel changelog "OpenID Connect (OIDC) Federation now generally available"); Team issuer mode recommended over Global. [as_of 2026-08-29, source: https://vercel.com/docs/oidc]
- Environment variable size limit: 64 KB total per deployment across Node.js/Python/Ruby/Go/PHP-community-runtime; Edge Functions/Middleware capped at 5 KB per variable. [as_of 2026-08-29, source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables]
- Node.js LTS pin for the greenfield Next.js 16 build: verify the currently-supported default in Vercel project Node.js version settings at build time rather than assuming a fixed number here [uncertain — exact currently-supported Node major version on Vercel not independently re-verified in this pass].
**limits_quotas_pricing** _Hard limits, quotas, free-tier cliffs, and pricing units with as_of dates and primary URLs; estimates marked [uncertain]_:

- item: Environment variables per environment per project | value: 1000 max | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/limits
- item: Environment variables total size per deployment (all vars combined, also max size of any single var) | value: 64 KB (Node.js, Python, Ruby, Go, Java, .NET runtimes) | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables
- item: Environment variable size on Edge Functions/Middleware (edge runtime) | value: 5 KB per variable | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables
- item: Vercel Projects connected per Git repository | value: 25 (Hobby), 150 (Pro), Custom (Enterprise) | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/limits
- item: Build Time per Deployment | value: 45 minutes (all plans) | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/limits
- item: Concurrent Deployments (builds) | value: 1 (Hobby), up to 500 (Pro, on-demand), Custom (Enterprise) | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/limits
- item: Deploy Hooks per project | value: 5 (Hobby/Pro), 10 (Enterprise) | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/limits
- item: Custom Environments per project | value: 1 (Pro), 12 (Enterprise); not available on Hobby | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/deployments/environments
- item: Sensitive environment variables — allowed target environments | value: Production and Preview only; not selectable for Development | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/sensitive-environment-variables
- item: Sensitive env var build-log redaction threshold | value: Values >=32 characters are replaced with [REDACTED] in build logs; VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET and VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN are always redacted regardless of length | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/sensitive-environment-variables
- item: Instant Rollback eligibility | value: Hobby: previous deployment only; Pro/Enterprise: any deployment previously aliased to a production domain | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/instant-rollback
- item: Password Protection / Advanced Deployment Protection | value: Included on Enterprise; $150/month add-on on Pro, minimum 30 days used before it can be disabled | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/deployment-protection
- item: Trusted IPs (protect only production, or restrict by IP at all) | value: Enterprise plan only | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/deployment-protection
- item: Related Projects (monorepo cross-project env linking) | value: Max 3 linked projects per app; same repository only; not supported for CLI deployments | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/monorepos
- item: Proxied request timeout | value: 120 seconds on all plans | as_of: 2026-08-29 | source: https://vercel.com/docs/limits
**deprecation_watch** _Services, runtimes, or plans to avoid for greenfield (sunset dates)_:
- (Legacy) Standard Protection and (Legacy) Pre-Production Deployments protection scopes are marked legacy in current docs; new projects should use Standard Protection or All Deployments instead. [as_of 2026-08-29, source: https://vercel.com/docs/deployment-protection]
- Legacy environment variable "Secret" type (pre-dating Sensitive env vars) is referenced in Vercel's changelog as being phased out in favor of the Config/Sensitive model — avoid the old `vercel secrets add` workflow for new projects. [uncertain — sunset date/details of the legacy secrets changelog entry not independently fetched in this pass]
- Global OIDC issuer mode is still supported but Team issuer mode is now the recommended default — new integrations should default to Team mode. [as_of 2026-08-29, source: https://vercel.com/docs/oidc]

### security

**identity_model** _How identity and authorization work for this surface (roles, OIDC/WIF, service accounts, tokens) and the no-long-lived-key rule_:
- Vercel's own actions (dashboard/API/CLI operations) are governed by team RBAC roles (Owner, Member, Developer, Billing, Viewer) plus project-level roles (Project Administrator) and Access Groups with extended permissions like "Full Production Deployment" — this governs who can roll back, promote, or edit env vars, not how the deployed app authenticates to external services.
- Application-to-cloud identity should use OIDC federation: Vercel's OIDC Identity Provider signs a short-lived JWT (`VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` in builds; `x-vercel-oidc-token` header in Functions) with claims identifying team, project, and environment (`sub` like `owner:team_example:project:my-app:environment:production`); AWS/GCP/Azure are configured to trust this IdP and exchange the token for short-lived native credentials (AWS: `sts:AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` against an IAM role; GCP: Workload Identity Federation pool/provider).
- Team (recommended) issuer mode scopes the issuer URL per-team (`https://oidc.vercel.com/team_example`); Global mode uses a shared generic issuer URL and is less specific for trust-policy scoping.
- Function-time OIDC tokens are reused for up to 90 minutes with a 2-hour TTL (the remaining 30 minutes covers max Function execution duration); build-time tokens are generated fresh per build.
- Local development can either pull the OIDC token via `vercel env pull` (short-lived, tied to the linked project) or the cloud provider can be configured to separately trust local/dev identities so no long-lived key is ever written to a laptop.
- No-long-lived-key rule: for AWS/GCP resources reachable via OIDC federation, Vercel env vars should hold only a role ARN / workload identity provider resource name (not a secret) — the actual credential is minted per-request/per-build and expires automatically.
**threats** _Security threats for this surface_:
- Long-lived cloud credentials (AWS access keys, GCP service-account credential material) stored as Vercel env vars are exfiltrated via a dependency compromise, SSRF, or log leak, granting durable unauthorized cloud access.
- A Sensitive-flagged env var is skipped for a genuinely secret value (developer forgets to toggle Sensitive), leaving it readable by any team member with project access and exportable via `vercel env pull` or the dashboard.
- Preview deployments carrying Production-equivalent credentials are exposed via a shared/leaked preview URL, since preview URLs are unauthenticated by default unless Deployment Protection is enabled.
- A Protection Bypass secret is leaked (committed to a repo, logged in a third-party webhook URL, embedded in client-side code) and used to bypass Vercel Authentication, Password Protection, and Trusted IPs across every deployment in the project until revoked.
- An overly permissive OIDC trust policy (`sub` wildcarded across environments or projects, or the `aud` check dropped) lets any Vercel deployment in the team assume a role intended for production-only access, widening blast radius from a compromised preview deployment.
- Fork-based pull requests from public repos attempt to trigger a deployment carrying real secrets; Vercel requires authorization for external fork PRs, but a misconfigured collaboration setting could still expose secrets to an untrusted contributor's preview build.
- Rollback used without checking migration state exposes the app to data corruption or crashes if the reverted code assumes an older/incompatible schema than what's currently live in Supabase.
- Malicious or compromised Git integration app permissions (overly broad GitHub App scope) could read/write repository content beyond what's needed for deploy triggers.
**mitigations** _Required mitigations and guardrails_:
- Default every AWS/GCP-reaching Function to OIDC federation; treat any newly-added static cloud credential in an env var as a review-blocking finding.
- Enforce the team-wide "Enforce Sensitive Environment Variables" policy (Owner-level Security & Privacy setting) so all newly created Production/Preview env vars are Sensitive by default, removing the human toggle-forgetting failure mode.
- Scope OIDC trust policy `sub` conditions per environment (`environment:production` vs `environment:preview`) and per project rather than wildcarding across the whole team; pin `aud` to the expected value and rotate/re-validate after any project rename.
- Keep Deployment Protection enabled (Standard Protection minimum) on every non-Hobby project by default via team-wide Deployment Protection defaults, so new projects don't launch unauthenticated.
- Issue a distinct Protection Bypass secret per automation consumer (CI, Playwright, uptime monitor, webhook) so any one leak can be revoked without breaking the others; store the secret only as a CI secret / Vercel-managed `VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET`, never in a committed file.
- Never target Preview environments with production-equivalent Stripe/Supabase keys; use an isolated Supabase branch/staging project and Stripe test-mode keys as the local module already specifies.
- Rely on Vercel's fork-PR authorization gate (comment-based approval) and keep it enabled; do not add all external contributors as team members purely to skip the check.
- Before any Instant Rollback, check for migrations applied since the target deployment; prefer forward-fix + redeploy whenever a destructive/irreversible migration has run, per the existing local module guidance (no conflict — reinforced by Vercel's own note that rollback does not touch env vars or external DB state).
- Explicitly set `AWS_REGION` as a project env var rather than relying on the Function's execution region, to keep OIDC-authenticated AWS calls deterministic under multi-region routing/failover.
- Rotate the Protection Bypass secret and redeploy after any suspected leak (Vercel invalidates the value embedded in prior deployments only upon redeploy, so rotation alone is insufficient — a redeploy is required to fully propagate).
**agent_codegen_policy** _Must-generate / must-not for coding agents touching this surface_:
- Must generate OIDC-based credential exchange code (`@vercel/oidc-aws-credentials-provider` or GCP Workload Identity equivalent) whenever a Vercel Function needs AWS/GCP access — must not generate code that reads static AWS/GCP credential material from an env var.
- Must scope any new environment variable to the correct environment(s) explicitly (never default to "all environments" for a credential) and must flag/ask before creating a non-Sensitive env var whose name or context implies a secret (contains `KEY`, `SECRET`, `TOKEN`, `PASSWORD`, `SERVICE_ROLE`).
- Must not write example/config code containing real team slugs, project IDs, role ARNs, or hostnames — placeholders only (`my-app.vercel.app`, `prj_example`, `team_example`, `arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/example-role`).
- Must not hardcode a Protection Bypass secret, session cookie, or any deployment-protection credential into source, `vercel.json`, or client-side code — must reference it via `process.env.VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET` sourced from CI secrets.
- Must not suggest disabling Deployment Protection to fix a failing automated test; must instead wire up Protection Bypass headers/query params.
- Must not generate a rollback-and-forget flow; any rollback automation must include a migration-state check or a manual confirmation step before invoking `vercel promote`/Instant Rollback.
- Must set up one Vercel Project + Root Directory per deployable app in a monorepo scaffold, not a single project building multiple apps via a custom script.
- Must default new env-var-touching code to `vercel env pull` for local parity instead of manually duplicating `.env.local` values, and must ensure `.env.local`/`.env*.local` remain gitignored.
- Should default to Team issuer mode when scaffolding new OIDC configuration, and should scope trust-policy `sub` conditions per environment rather than using a wildcard example verbatim in production config.

### quality

**ci_gates** _CI checks and deploy gates (plan/apply review, policy, drift, smoke)_:
- CI must fail if a required env var is missing for the target environment (fail fast rather than deploying with `undefined` config).
- Pre-deploy check: verify no plaintext-looking secret patterns (cloud key IDs, private-key-shaped headers, JWT-shaped strings assigned to non-Sensitive vars) are being added via `vercel env add`/API without the `sensitive` type.
- Gate merges to the production branch on `npm run build` (or equivalent) passing locally/in CI before merge, per existing local module guidance — no conflict, reinforced.
- E2E/Playwright smoke suite must run against the Preview deployment URL using Protection Bypass headers before a PR is approved to merge to the production branch.
- Migration-and-deploy should be split into reviewable steps (schema migration PR, then app-code PR) so that Instant Rollback of the app code remains safe without automatically reverting schema state — matches existing local module anti-pattern guidance.
- For monorepos, verify `turbo-ignore`/Ignored Build Step or automatic unaffected-project skipping is actually skipping correctly (spot-check build logs) rather than assuming it silently works after initial setup.
**observability** _Logs, metrics, traces, alerts; OTel export path; what to alert on_:
- Vercel Activity Log records every masked/redacted Sensitive env var event (key, project, deployment — not value) — alert on unexpected spikes in redaction events as a possible leak-attempt signal.
- Monitor build logs for `[REDACTED]` markers appearing where they shouldn't (e.g., a var expected to be short/non-sensitive suddenly redacted) as a signal of misclassified data.
- Track deployment protection bypass usage patterns (which automation consumer, how often) via the dashboard; alert on bypass secret usage from unexpected IP ranges or off-hours patterns.
- Monitor Instant Rollback events (who, when, from/to deployment) since a rollback silently disables auto-assignment of production domains — alert if `main` has new commits merged while the project is in a rolled-back state (a real ship risk).
- For OIDC federation, monitor AWS CloudTrail / GCP Cloud Audit Logs for `AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` / STS token exchanges tied to the Vercel OIDC provider, watching for calls from unexpected `sub` claims (wrong project/environment).
- Track build time trending toward the 45-minute cap and concurrent-build queueing (Hobby: 1 concurrent build) as an early warning before builds start failing outright.
**cost_guardrails** _Budgets, alerts, egress/NAT/bandwidth traps, spend caps, and the first three cost surprises teams hit_:
- Advanced Deployment Protection (Password Protection, Private Production Deployments, Protection Exceptions) is a flat $150/month Pro add-on with a mandatory 30-day minimum before disabling — budget for this explicitly rather than discovering it mid-cycle.
- Custom Environments are free on Pro/Enterprise but capped (1/12 per project) — don't architect around unlimited staging environments per project.
- Concurrent build capacity (Hobby: 1, Pro: up to 500 on-demand) directly affects CI queue time for monorepos with many projects; large monorepos on Hobby will serialize builds and can silently slow down every PR.
- The 45-minute build time cap per deployment (all plans) is a hard wall, not a billed overage — a growing Python service build approaching this ceiling needs build optimization (caching, fewer install steps) before it becomes an outage, not a bigger plan.
- Vercel Projects Connected per Git Repository (25 Hobby / 150 Pro) caps how many monorepo apps/services can live in one repo before requiring an Enterprise custom limit — plan monorepo growth against this ceiling.
- Deploy Hooks (5 on Hobby/Pro, 10 Enterprise) and Cron Jobs (100/project) are modest per-project ceilings that can be exhausted by ad hoc automation scripts; consolidate rather than provisioning a new hook per throwaway integration.
- First three cost/quota surprises teams hit in this surface: (1) discovering Password Protection/Trusted IPs require Enterprise or the $150/mo add-on after assuming Pro included them; (2) hitting the 1000-env-vars-per-environment or 64KB total-size ceiling when an integration auto-injects many vars; (3) realizing Instant Rollback eligibility is limited to "previous deployment only" on Hobby, forcing an upgrade to Pro to roll back further during an incident.
**skill_scan_signals** _Positive/negative signals engineer-scan and check-oss-leaks should flag for this surface (identifier shapes, config smells)_:
- Positive: env vars referencing `process.env.AWS_ROLE_ARN` + `@vercel/oidc-aws-credentials-provider` (OIDC pattern present); `VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET` referenced only from CI/test config, never hardcoded; env var names containing `KEY|SECRET|TOKEN|PASSWORD` cross-checked against dashboard Sensitive flag.
- Negative/flag: literal static cloud credential material (access key/secret pairs, PEM-formatted service-account key blocks) assigned to a Vercel env var or committed `.env` file.
- Negative/flag: `x-vercel-protection-bypass=` value embedded directly in a URL string in source code or a checked-in webhook config rather than interpolated from an env var at deploy/config time.
- Negative/flag: `vercel.json` or CI config disabling Deployment Protection (or setting protection scope to None) alongside a comment referencing tests failing — likely protection-was-disabled-to-unblock-CI anti-pattern.
- Negative/flag: a single Vercel Project's `buildCommand` invoking multiple app directories (e.g. building several sub-apps sequentially in one command) instead of separate projects with Root Directory — monorepo anti-pattern.
- Negative/flag: identical env var values present across Production and Preview for anything named like a credential (signal of shared secrets across environments).
- Positive: `turbo-ignore` or documented custom Ignored Build Step present in a monorepo lacking workspace-manifest-based unaffected-project skipping eligibility.

### actionability

**skill_module_bullets** _Bullets that must land in the target references module_:
- Production deploys trigger on push/merge to the production branch (default `main`); every other branch, PR, or plain `vercel` CLI call creates a Preview deployment with its own URL — this is automatic, no config needed.
- Env vars are scoped per environment (Production/Preview/Development, plus Pro/Enterprise Custom Environments) and can be branch-specific within Preview (CLI ≥22.0.0); never assume one var value applies everywhere.
- Mark any credential-shaped Production/Preview env var as Sensitive — it becomes write-only/unreadable after creation, is redacted in build logs above 32 chars, and cannot be selected for Development.
- For AWS/GCP access from Vercel Functions, default to OIDC federation (`VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` → AWS `AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` / GCP Workload Identity Federation) instead of static keys — this is the no-long-lived-key pattern for this stack.
- Deployment Protection defaults: Standard Protection (protects everything except the live production domain) is available on all plans; All Deployments, Password Protection, Trusted IPs, and Passport require Pro add-on or Enterprise.
- Use a Protection Bypass secret (`VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET`, header `x-vercel-protection-bypass`) for CI/E2E/webhook automation instead of disabling protection.
- Instant Rollback re-aliases domains to a prior production deployment without a rebuild; it does not revert env var changes or database migrations, and it disables auto-assignment of production domains until you `vercel promote` (or Undo Rollback) to resume normal deploys.
- Hobby can only roll back to the immediately previous deployment; Pro/Enterprise can roll back to any deployment that was ever aliased to production.
- Monorepos: one Vercel Project per app with its own Root Directory; Vercel auto-skips builds for unaffected workspace packages (npm/yarn/pnpm/Bun workspaces with unique package names and declared internal deps); fall back to a custom Ignored Build Step (e.g. `turbo query`) when auto-skip requirements aren't met.
- Environment variable hard limits: 1000 vars per environment per project, 64 KB total size per deployment (5 KB per var on Edge/Middleware) — plan large JWTs/certs against this.
**checklist** _Pre-ship checklist for this surface_:

- Production branch is set correctly and matches the team's intended release branch.
- Every credential-shaped Production/Preview env var is flagged Sensitive; team-wide Sensitive enforcement policy considered.
- No static long-lived AWS/GCP credential material exists in any Vercel env var; OIDC federation is wired for any function needing cloud access, with AWS_REGION pinned explicitly.
- Preview environment uses test-mode Stripe keys and an isolated/staging Supabase project or branch — never production keys.
- Deployment Protection is enabled at least at Standard Protection with Vercel Authentication on any non-fully-public project.
- A distinct Protection Bypass secret exists per automation consumer (CI, Playwright, webhook) and is referenced only via env var, never hardcoded.
- Rollback runbook confirmed: check for migrations applied since the rollback target before using Instant Rollback; prefer forward-fix if a destructive migration ran.
- Post-rollback, `vercel promote`/Undo Rollback plan is understood so auto-assignment of production domains isn't left disabled indefinitely.
- Monorepo apps each have their own Vercel Project and Root Directory; unaffected-build skipping verified working (or Ignored Build Step configured as fallback).
- Env var total size per deployment checked against the 64 KB cap (5 KB per var on Edge/Middleware) if large JWTs/certs are in use.
- Team RBAC reviewed so only intended roles (Owner/Project Administrator/Full Production Deployment access-group members) can roll back or edit Production env vars.
- `vercel env pull` used for local parity; `.env.local`/`.env*.local` confirmed gitignored.

### evidence

- **source_tier**: official
- **confidence**: high
- **conflicts_noted**: No direct contradiction found with the existing local module. The local module's Node 24 LTS claim and 'Node 20 is EOL' framing could not be independently re-verified against a fetched Vercel runtime-support page in this pass (Vercel's build/runtime version pages were not fetched); treat that specific version number as still authoritative from the local module but unverified here — flagged in uncertain. The local module's rollback guidance ('rollback alone may be insufficient if migration was applied, prefer forward-fix') is fully corroborated by Vercel's own docs, which explicitly state Instant Rollback does not update/rebuild environment variables and reverts cron jobs to the rolled-back deployment's state, with no mention of any migration/database rollback — reinforces rather than conflicts. The local module's guidance to protect preview URLs via 'Vercel authentication or IP allowlist' is consistent with current docs, except Trusted IPs (the IP-allowlist mechanism) is Enterprise-only per current docs — worth noting for Pro-plan teams that IP allowlisting isn't available to them.

## Vercel domains, security, observability — DNS/domains, WAF/firewall, bot management, headers, logs/drains, OTel, Speed Insights

### identity

- **name**: Vercel domains, security, observability — DNS/domains, WAF/firewall, bot management, headers, logs/drains, OTel, Speed Insights
- **item_id**: vercel-domains-security-observability
- **cluster**: vercel
- **short_definition**: Covers the surface area of Vercel that sits between DNS and the running app on a Next.js 16 + Python services + Supabase stack: how custom domains and DNS (nameservers vs external CNAME/A/ALIAS records) get traffic to a project, how the Vercel Firewall (platform DDoS mitigation, WAF custom rules, IP blocking, managed rulesets, bot management, Attack Mode) filters that traffic before it reaches app code, how response headers (CSP/HSTS/frame options) get set via vercel.json or next.config.js, and how runtime signal (logs, OpenTelemetry traces, Speed Insights Core Web Vitals) gets out of Vercel via Drains to external sinks like Datadog/Honeycomb/Grafana, composing with an external error monitor (Sentry) rather than replacing it.
**scope_in** _What this item covers_:
- Adding/verifying custom domains (apex and subdomain), Vercel nameservers vs external DNS, ALIAS/A/CNAME/HTTPS record choices, wildcard domains, SSL cert issuance and CAA
- Vercel Firewall: platform-wide DDoS mitigation (free, automatic), WAF custom rules, IP blocking, rate limiting, managed rulesets (OWASP CRS, Bot Protection, AI Bots), Attack Mode, rule execution order
- Bot management/verification (verified bots directory, Web Bot Auth, AI crawler control)
- Security response headers set via vercel.json headers[] or Next.js next.config.js headers(), CSP construction for Next.js (inline scripts/nonces, hydration script tags), HSTS, X-Frame-Options
- Drains (formerly "log drains"): logs, OTel traces, Speed Insights, Web Analytics, Audit Logs, Connect events — destinations, schemas, security (signatures), pricing
- OpenTelemetry instrumentation via @vercel/otel, context propagation, custom spans, sampling interaction with upstream traceparent
- Speed Insights (Core Web Vitals RUM) vs Web Analytics (page views/custom events) vs external RUM/error tools
**scope_out** _Explicit non-goals and adjacent items that own them_:
- Vercel project/build configuration, framework presets, and Edge/Node runtime selection — owned by a deploy-ops/runtime item
- Supabase RLS, Postgres connection pooling, and auth — owned by the Supabase cluster items
- General CI/CD pipeline design and preview-deployment workflows — owned by deploy-ops.md
- AWS/GCP OIDC federation mechanics for Python services — owned by the aws/gcp identity items; this item only notes the same identity-first preference for drain/export auth
- Application-level error boundary code and Sentry SDK setup details — owned by an application observability item; this item only defines the division of responsibility between Sentry and Vercel-native tools
- **priority**: P1

### practice

**defaults** _Opinionated greenfield defaults for 2026 (what to pick without deliberation)_:
- Buy or transfer the apex domain and use Vercel nameservers when Vercel is the only place the domain needs to resolve (simplest, enables wildcard domains and auto-managed SSL/CAA) — use external DNS (A/ALIAS + CNAME) only when other records (mail, other subdomains on other providers) must live outside Vercel.
- For apex domain on external DNS: A record to the IP shown on the project's Domains settings (commonly `76.76.21.21`, but always read the value Vercel shows, do not hardcode) or an ALIAS/ANAME/HTTPS record if the DNS provider supports one; add `www` as a CNAME to `cname.vercel-dns.com` (or the value Vercel provides) and redirect apex→www or www→apex, not both live.
- Turn on the Vercel WAF and DDoS mitigation from day one — DDoS mitigation and IP blocking are free on every plan including Hobby; add a handful of custom rules for auth/login/checkout routes before launch, not after an incident.
- Enable the Bot Protection managed ruleset in challenge mode for interactive routes and the AI Bots managed ruleset (log first, then deny) once you have a scraping/training-data policy — do not put Cloudflare or another reverse proxy in front of Vercel, it breaks bot-signal accuracy.
- Set security headers (CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options/frame-ancestors, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy) in `next.config.js` headers() or `vercel.json`, and verify with securityheaders.com post-deploy per the existing deploy-ops smoke check.
- Instrument with `@vercel/otel` from day one on Pro+ (adds negligible cost, high debugging leverage); export traces via a Trace Drain to your OTel collector/Datadog/Honeycomb/Grafana rather than only viewing in the Vercel dashboard.
- Keep Sentry (or equivalent) as the system of record for application errors/stack traces; use Vercel Speed Insights for Core Web Vitals RUM and Web Analytics for traffic — do not try to make Speed Insights replace error monitoring, it does not capture JS exceptions.
- Log Drains and Trace Drains require Pro or Enterprise; Hobby cannot export logs/traces off-platform, only view the last runtime log window in-dashboard — budget for Pro before you need real incident forensics.
**decision_gates** _When to use / when not / which alternative (incl. the Supabase, Vercel, and VPS alternatives)_:
- Use Vercel nameservers when: buying the domain through Vercel, you want wildcard subdomains (wildcard domains REQUIRE the nameservers method for cert issuance), or you want Vercel to manage all DNS/SSL/renewals in one place.
- Use external DNS (A/ALIAS/CNAME) when: the domain's mail (MX) or other subdomains must stay on a different DNS provider you don't want to migrate, or org policy centralizes DNS in a single external provider (e.g. Route 53/Cloudflare DNS) for auditability.
- Use Vercel WAF custom rules for: request-shape filtering (path, header, query, geo, JA3/JA4 fingerprint) in front of your own app logic; use Supabase RLS/Postgres policies for authorization decisions — WAF is network-edge, not app-level authz.
- Use the Bot Protection managed ruleset (challenge) for pages hit by scrapers/credential-stuffing (login, signup, checkout); use IP blocking only for known-bad, static IP/CIDR sources — it doesn't scale to distributed botnets, pair with rate limiting or Attack Mode instead.
- Use Attack Mode manually during an active volumetric attack for extra JS-challenge coverage; it is a manual toggle, not a standing default — leaving it on permanently degrades UX for legitimate users.
- Use Drains + your own SIEM/observability stack once you need >7-30 day retention, cross-service correlation, or compliance log retention — Vercel's own dashboards are fine pre-launch but are not a durable log store.
- Use Sentry for uncaught exceptions/stack traces/release health; use Vercel Speed Insights for field Core Web Vitals (LCP/INP/CLS) and Web Analytics for pageviews/conversion funnels — these are complementary, not substitutes for each other.
- If you need managed rulesets (OWASP CRS) or account-level IP blocking beyond small numbers, or JA3 legacy fingerprint matching, you need Enterprise — Pro caps custom rules at 40 and has no managed rulesets.
**do_dont** _Concrete do and don't lists_:

- Do read the exact A-record IP and CNAME target from the project's own Domains settings screen rather than hardcoding `76.76.21.21` from memory or an old blog post — the value can differ per project/region and Vercel may rotate it.
- Do add both the apex and `www` as separate domain entries in Project Settings > Domains and configure a redirect between them so only one is canonical.
- Don't put Cloudflare, Azure Front Door, or another reverse proxy in front of a Vercel deployment if you plan to use Bot Protection — it masks the signals bot detection relies on and causes repeated re-challenges for real users.
- Do enable DDoS mitigation and basic WAF custom rules before launch, not reactively after a traffic spike — DDoS mitigation is free and automatic but WAF custom rules need to be authored.
- Don't rely on IP blocking alone as a bot/attack defense — CIDR-based blocking doesn't scale past small, known-bad ranges; use rate limiting, challenge actions, or managed rulesets for distributed sources.
- Do set CSP with a nonce-based strategy for Next.js inline/hydration scripts (via middleware generating a per-request nonce placed on `<Script>` and the CSP header) rather than `unsafe-inline`, which defeats the header's purpose.
- Don't ship `Strict-Transport-Security` with a long max-age before verifying HTTPS works correctly on every subdomain you own — HSTS preload mistakes are hard to reverse quickly.
- Do use Log/Trace Drains (Pro+) to export to a durable external sink for anything beyond short-term debugging; don't assume the in-dashboard log view is a retention/compliance solution.
- Don't configure OTel with a fully manual OpenTelemetry SDK setup if you want Session Tracing or Trace Drains — those features require using `@vercel/otel`.
- Do keep Sentry wired for exception/error tracking even after adopting Vercel Observability — Speed Insights and Web Analytics do not capture JS exceptions or backend error stack traces.
- Don't assume wildcard domains work with the CNAME/A-record method — wildcard domains must use the Vercel nameservers method because ACME challenge solving for wildcard certs requires Vercel to control DNS.
- Do shorten TTL to 60s about 24 hours before migrating an existing production domain's DNS to Vercel, then restore appropriate TTL after propagation confirms via a tool like whatsmydns.net.
**config_skeleton** _Minimal correct config or IaC skeleton with placeholder identifiers only (English comments ok)_:
```jsonc
// vercel.json — custom security headers (placeholders only)
{
  "headers": [
    {
      "source": "/(.*)",
      "headers": [
        {
          "key": "Strict-Transport-Security",
          "value": "max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload"
        },
        {
          "key": "X-Content-Type-Options",
          "value": "nosniff"
        },
        {
          "key": "X-Frame-Options",
          "value": "DENY"
        },
        {
          "key": "Referrer-Policy",
          "value": "strict-origin-when-cross-origin"
        },
        {
          "key": "Permissions-Policy",
          "value": "camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()"
        },
        {
          "key": "Content-Security-Policy",
          "value": "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-__NONCE_PLACEHOLDER__' https://cdn.example.com; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: https:; connect-src 'self' https://api.example.com; frame-ancestors 'none'"
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

```js
// next.config.js — equivalent headers via Next.js (alternative to vercel.json)
/** @type {import('next').NextConfig} */
const nextConfig = {
  async headers() {
    return [
      {
        source: '/(.*)',
        headers: [
          { key: 'X-Frame-Options', value: 'DENY' },
          { key: 'X-Content-Type-Options', value: 'nosniff' },
        ],
      },
    ];
  },
};
module.exports = nextConfig;
```

```jsonc
// Vercel WAF custom rule (conceptual placeholder — authored in dashboard/Terraform, not raw JSON in vercel.json)
// Rule: challenge non-browser traffic to /api/checkout
{
  "name": "challenge-checkout-bots",
  "conditions": [
    { "type": "path", "op": "eq", "value": "/api/checkout" },
    { "type": "header", "key": "user-agent", "op": "does_not_contain", "value": "Mozilla" }
  ],
  "action": "challenge"
}
```

```bash
# DNS records for a custom apex domain — placeholders only, read actual values from your project's Domains settings
# Apex (app.example.com used here as a stand-in for a root domain being connected)
Type: A       Host: @         Value: 76.76.21.21      TTL: 60   # or ALIAS/ANAME/HTTPS record if provider supports one
Type: CNAME   Host: www       Value: cname.vercel-dns.com   TTL: 60
# Vercel auto-manages a CAA record for Let's Encrypt at the zone apex when using Vercel nameservers
```

```bash
# Log/Trace Drain — custom HTTP endpoint (Pro/Enterprise). Prefer a short-lived bearer/signed-secret
# over a static long-lived API key where the sink supports rotation; verify the drain signature header
# on receipt (see /docs/drains/security) rather than trusting source IP alone.
DRAIN_ENDPOINT_URL="https://collector.example.com/vercel/ingest"
DRAIN_SECRET="<rotatable-shared-secret-placeholder>"   # used to verify x-vercel-signature
```

```ts
// instrumentation.ts — OpenTelemetry via @vercel/otel, exporting toward a placeholder OTLP collector
import { registerOTel } from '@vercel/otel';

export function register() {
  registerOTel({
    serviceName: 'my-app',
    // OTLP endpoint is normally configured via env vars read by the OTel SDK, e.g.:
    // OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=https://otel-collector.example.com:4318
    // OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_HEADERS="authorization=Bearer <short-lived-token-placeholder>"
    instrumentationConfig: {
      fetch: {
        propagateContextUrls: ['api.example.com'],
        ignoreUrls: ['internal-tool.example.com'],
      },
    },
  });
}
```
**failure_modes** _Top production failure modes and outage classes_:
- Apex domain shows "Invalid Configuration" because the A record points at a stale/incorrect IP copied from an old tutorial instead of the project's current Domains-tab value.
- Wildcard subdomain SSL never issues because the domain was connected via CNAME/A record instead of the required Vercel-nameservers method (ACME challenge for wildcard certs needs Vercel-controlled DNS).
- Email silently stops working after moving a domain's DNS to Vercel nameservers because MX records weren't re-added, since Vercel does not provide mail service by default.
- Bot Protection false-positives spike or detection quality degrades after inserting Cloudflare/another CDN in front of Vercel, because the reverse proxy masks the signals bot detection relies on.
- A CSP with `unsafe-inline` (added to silence Next.js hydration script errors) quietly neutralizes the header's XSS protection; the correct fix is a per-request nonce, which is more setup than teams budget for.
- HSTS with `preload` shipped before all subdomains reliably serve HTTPS, causing hard-to-reverse lockout for a subdomain that isn't ready.
- Custom Rules limit (40 on Pro) is hit while iterating on many narrow per-route rules instead of consolidating with match-condition logic, silently blocking further rule creation at ship time.
- Drains configured but the destination endpoint's signature/secret isn't verified, and the team relies on IP allowlisting alone, which can be spoofed or drift when Vercel's egress IPs change.
- Speed Insights sample rate left at 100% on a high-traffic Pro project causes unexpected overage cost (billed per-10k-events beyond the free tier) with no alerting configured.
- Manual OpenTelemetry SDK setup (bypassing `@vercel/otel`) silently loses Session Tracing and Trace Drains support, discovered only when trying to enable them later.
- DNS TTL left at a long value (e.g. 24h) during a domain migration causes hours of mixed old/new resolution and confusing intermittent outages during cutover.
**anti_patterns** _Patterns agents must not generate (static keys in CI, public buckets, open security groups, etc.)_:

- Hardcoding a Vercel apex A-record IP in infra-as-code or documentation instead of reading it from the live project Domains configuration or the REST API.
- Running Cloudflare (or any reverse proxy) in front of a Vercel deployment while expecting Bot Protection/WAF fingerprinting to work at full accuracy.
- Using `Content-Security-Policy` with `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` as a permanent fix for Next.js inline-script CSP violations instead of adopting nonces.
- Treating Vercel Speed Insights or Web Analytics as an error-monitoring replacement for Sentry — they do not capture unhandled exceptions or stack traces.
- Relying solely on static, long-lived API keys/bearer tokens for log/trace drain destinations when the sink supports short-lived credentials or signed-request verification.
- Leaving Attack Mode or aggressive challenge rulesets permanently enabled as a default posture rather than a temporary response to an active incident.
- Assuming the in-dashboard log/firewall view is a durable audit trail; it is not a substitute for Drains to an external SIEM when compliance retention is required.
- Configuring a wildcard domain via CNAME/A record and being surprised the SSL certificate never provisions.

### tooling

**tools** _Primary CLIs/SDKs/consoles and alternatives_:

- Vercel Dashboard — Domains, Firewall, Drains, Speed Insights, Observability tabs (primary UI for all items in this scope)
- Vercel CLI — `vercel domains`, `vercel dns`, firewall management commands
- Vercel REST API / Terraform provider — programmatic domain, DNS record, and firewall rule management
- @vercel/otel + @opentelemetry/api — Node/Next.js OpenTelemetry instrumentation package
- @vercel/speed-insights — Core Web Vitals RUM client package
- @vercel/analytics — Web Analytics client package
- securityheaders.com — external header audit tool referenced by the existing deploy-ops smoke check
- whatsmydns.net — DNS propagation checker
- Sentry — external error monitoring (system of record for exceptions, complements rather than duplicates Vercel Observability)
- External OTel sinks — Datadog, Honeycomb, Grafana Cloud/Tempo, Dash0, Braintrust (native Drain integrations exist for some)
- Alternative/adjacent: Cloudflare (DNS-only mode is compatible; reverse-proxy/orange-cloud mode is explicitly discouraged in front of Vercel)

**deprecation_watch** _Services, runtimes, or plans to avoid for greenfield (sunset dates)_:
- "Log Drains" as a standalone product name is superseded by the unified "Drains" product covering logs, traces, Speed Insights, analytics, Connect events, and audit logs under one REST API and `schemas` property — new integrations should target the Drains API, not the deprecated Configurable Log Drain endpoint.
- The REST API endpoint "Creates a Configurable Log Drain" is explicitly marked deprecated in Vercel's own docs; use the current Drains creation endpoint instead.
- Custom SIEM Log Streaming is being migrated to Audit Log Drains (Enterprise) — Vercel publishes a dedicated migration guide, implying the older streaming mechanism is on its way out.
- Manual/raw OpenTelemetry SDK configuration (bypassing `@vercel/otel`) is a supported but functionally limited path — Session Tracing and Trace Drains require `@vercel/otel`, so treat hand-rolled OTel setup as a dead end for teams wanting full observability integration.

### security

**identity_model** _How identity and authorization work for this surface (roles, OIDC/WIF, service accounts, tokens) and the no-long-lived-key rule_:
- Domain/DNS management authorization is scoped to the Vercel team that owns the domain; adding a domain already claimed under a different team requires a TXT-record ownership verification step rather than any user-identity check.
- Firewall, WAF, and Drains configuration require specific Vercel dashboard permission scopes ("Vercel WAF", "DDoS Mitigation", "Drains", "Bot protection managed ruleset", "AI bots managed ruleset", "Speed Insights" are each called out as distinct permission-gated features in the docs) — apply least-privilege team roles rather than giving every collaborator Owner/Admin.
- Programmatic WAF/domain/DNS management goes through the Vercel REST API using a team Access Token (integration token) or the Terraform provider; these are Vercel platform credentials, not customer-cloud IAM, so store them as CI secrets with the narrowest applicable scope and rotate them — they are not currently exchangeable via OIDC federation the way AWS/GCP roles are.
- For the export side (Drains to external sinks, OTel to a collector), prefer the destination's short-lived/rotatable auth (signed webhook secrets, OIDC-issued tokens where the sink is AWS/GCP-backed, e.g. an S3 audit-log drain assumed via a role) over a static long-lived API key baked into the Drain config, consistent with the stack's identity-first, no-long-lived-keys posture; verify inbound Drain payload signatures (Vercel's `x-vercel-signature`-style header) rather than trusting source IP allowlisting alone.
- Bot verification for inbound traffic (Googlebot, etc.) is IP-range + reverse-DNS + optional cryptographic (Web Bot Auth / HTTP Message Signatures, RFC 9421) — this is about authenticating third-party crawlers hitting you, not your own service identity.
**threats** _Security threats for this surface_:
- DNS hijacking/misconfiguration: a stale or attacker-modified A/CNAME record silently redirects your domain's traffic off Vercel entirely.
- Domain takeover via an unclaimed `.vercel.app` or custom subdomain left dangling (CNAME pointing to a deleted/unclaimed Vercel project) — a classic subdomain-takeover vector.
- Volumetric/DDoS and TCP flood attacks against the platform edge (mitigated automatically, but still a threat model to be aware of for capacity planning).
- Credential stuffing / scraping / content-scraping bots hitting login, checkout, or content-heavy routes.
- AI/LLM training-data crawlers scraping proprietary content without permission if the AI Bots ruleset is left in default "Allow" mode.
- Reflected/stored XSS mitigated only partially if CSP is missing or uses `unsafe-inline`, especially with Next.js's inline hydration scripts.
- Clickjacking if `X-Frame-Options`/`frame-ancestors` is absent.
- Drain/export endpoint spoofing — a malicious actor sending fake Vercel-looking log/trace payloads to your ingestion endpoint if signature verification isn't enforced.
- Sensitive data leakage into logs/traces sent to third-party Drain destinations (PII, tokens) if application code logs indiscriminately before export.
- Reverse-proxy-in-front-of-Vercel setups (e.g. Cloudflare orange-cloud) undermining WAF/bot-detection signal integrity, effectively creating a blind spot.
**mitigations** _Required mitigations and guardrails_:
- Verify DNS record values against the live Vercel project Domains settings before every change; monitor DNS with an external checker and alert on unexpected changes.
- Remove/redirect any custom domain or subdomain no longer pointing at an active Vercel project to close subdomain-takeover risk; periodically audit team Domains list for orphaned entries.
- Enable DDoS mitigation (automatic/free) and add WAF custom rules with rate limiting on sensitive routes (auth, checkout, search) before launch.
- Turn on Bot Protection managed ruleset in challenge mode for interactive/auth routes, and decide explicitly (log or deny) for the AI Bots managed ruleset rather than leaving it in default Allow.
- Never deploy a reverse proxy in front of Vercel if bot-detection accuracy matters; if a CDN is required, keep DNS-only mode.
- Build CSP with per-request nonces for Next.js inline/hydration scripts; set HSTS (`includeSubDomains`), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options`/`frame-ancestors`, and `Referrer-Policy` via vercel.json or next.config.js headers().
- Verify Drain payload signatures at the receiving endpoint (per /docs/drains/security) instead of relying on IP allowlisting alone; scope any static Drain secret narrowly and rotate it periodically; prefer OIDC/short-lived credentials for cloud-backed sinks (e.g. S3 audit-log drain via an assumed role) over static keys.
- Scrub/redact sensitive fields in application logs before they reach Drains; treat exported logs/traces as leaving your trust boundary.
- Use Firewall Observability + alerts to get notified of anomalous traffic patterns in near real time rather than discovering them after the fact in Drains only.
- Keep Sentry (or an equivalent APM/error tool) wired independently of Vercel Observability so an outage or misconfiguration in one doesn't blind you to the other.
**agent_codegen_policy** _Must-generate / must-not for coding agents touching this surface_:
- MUST read the actual A-record/CNAME target from the live project's Domains settings or REST API when generating DNS setup instructions or IaC — never hardcode a specific IP as universally correct.
- MUST default to nonce-based CSP generation for any Next.js app with inline or injected `<Script>` tags; MUST NOT emit `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` as a "quick fix" without flagging it as a security downgrade.
- MUST NOT scaffold a reverse proxy (Cloudflare proxy mode, custom nginx in front) ahead of a Vercel deployment when Bot Protection/WAF is in use, without an explicit warning about signal degradation.
- MUST default new custom-header configs to include HSTS, X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options/frame-ancestors, and Referrer-Policy, holding CSP for a follow-up pass with the team's actual script/style origins listed as placeholders.
- MUST use `@vercel/otel`'s `registerOTel()` rather than hand-rolled OpenTelemetry SDK bootstrapping, to preserve Session Tracing/Trace Drains compatibility.
- MUST NOT embed static long-lived API keys/tokens directly in Drain endpoint configs or repo code when the target sink supports short-lived tokens, signed requests, or OIDC-based auth — surface this as a config decision requiring a secret manager or CI secret, not a hardcoded value.
- MUST generate only placeholder identifiers (e.g. `app.example.com`, `prj_example`, `my-app.vercel.app`) in any example config, never a real domain, team, or project id.
- SHOULD recommend keeping an external error monitor (Sentry) wired alongside Vercel Speed Insights/Web Analytics rather than proposing removal of Sentry in favor of Vercel-native tools, since they cover different signal types.

### quality

**ci_gates** _CI checks and deploy gates (plan/apply review, policy, drift, smoke)_:
- Post-deploy smoke check should include a headers audit (securityheaders.com or an equivalent automated header-diff check) as already noted in deploy-ops.md, extended to explicitly assert CSP, HSTS, and X-Frame-Options presence.
- A DNS/domain health check (resolve apex + www, confirm cert validity and expected redirect) should run after any domain-related config change, not just after initial setup.
- WAF/Firewall rule changes should go through the same review gate as other infra changes (diff custom rules in PR/Terraform plan before applying) given the 300ms global propagation and "instant rollback" capability exists precisely because misconfigurations can be immediate and broad.
- Drain endpoint configuration changes should be validated via the REST API's drain-delivery validation endpoint before going live, to catch destination auth/format errors pre-flight.
- OTel instrumentation changes should be verified in a preview deployment (check that traces appear in the configured Trace Drain or collector) before promoting to production.
**observability** _Logs, metrics, traces, alerts; OTel export path; what to alert on_:
- Firewall Observability (dashboard "Firewall" tab) provides live traffic monitoring, per-rule hit counts, and an audit log with instant rollback to prior firewall configurations.
- Team-level Monitoring/query feature lets you build custom queries across projects for traffic patterns.
- Firewall alerts allow near-real-time notification on suspicious traffic; wire these to the team's existing alerting channel.
- Drains export Logs (runtime/build/static), Traces (OTel format), Speed Insights, Web Analytics, Connect events, and Audit Logs to custom HTTP endpoints or native integrations (Datadog, Splunk, Panther, S3, Dash0, Braintrust, etc.) — pick Trace Drains specifically for OTel spans headed to an external APM.
- @vercel/otel + @opentelemetry/api gives function-level tracing with context propagation across `fetch` calls; Edge runtime does not support custom spans — plan Node runtime for functions needing rich custom instrumentation.
- Speed Insights dashboard shows Real Experience Score, FCP, LCP by device/environment/route with P75/P90/P95/P99 views — this is field RUM data, not synthetic Lighthouse; use it to catch route-level regressions.
- Sentry (external, not Vercel-native) remains the source of truth for exceptions, stack traces, and release health per the existing deploy-ops.md posture — do not expect Vercel Observability to replace it.
- Alert on: unexpected WAF rule-hit spikes, Drain delivery failures, Speed Insights event volume nearing plan cap, and Sentry error-rate thresholds — a single dashboard rarely covers all of these, so route them to a shared alerting surface.
**cost_guardrails** _Budgets, alerts, egress/NAT/bandwidth traps, spend caps, and the first three cost surprises teams hit_:
- Drains billed per GB of uncompressed JSON serialization ($0.50/GB) regardless of data type — high-volume logs/traces on a busy service can surprise a team that assumed "just logging" is free; set a Spend Management cap.
- Speed Insights on Pro carries a $10/project/month base fee plus per-event overage ($0.65/10k events) — enabling it reflexively across many projects adds up; use the sample rate and `beforeSend` filtering to control volume.
- WAF managed rulesets and rate limiting are priced features beyond the free baseline (DDoS mitigation, IP blocking, and custom rules are free) — read /docs/vercel-firewall/vercel-waf/usage-and-pricing before assuming all Firewall features are free.
- The three most common early cost surprises: (1) enabling Drains broadly without sampling and getting billed on full log volume, (2) enabling Speed Insights on every project without adjusting sample rate, (3) hitting the Pro WAF Custom Rules cap (40) while iterating and needing an Enterprise upgrade sooner than planned to add managed rulesets.
- Set up Spend Management (webhook or auto-pause) at the team level so Drains/Speed Insights overage triggers a notification or pause rather than an unbounded bill.
- Domain costs: buying through Vercel matches registrar pricing 1:1 (no markup claimed), and Pro teams get one free first-year domain on select TLDs (.online/.site/.space/.store/.tech/.website) — factor the post-year-one renewal cost into budgeting.
**skill_scan_signals** _Positive/negative signals engineer-scan and check-oss-leaks should flag for this surface (identifier shapes, config smells)_:
- Positive: vercel.json or next.config.js headers() block present with CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options/frame-ancestors, Referrer-Policy set.
- Positive: `@vercel/otel` present in package.json and an `instrumentation.ts`/`.js` calling `registerOTel`.
- Positive: DNS/domain examples in docs/config using placeholder-style domains rather than a real production hostname.
- Negative: CSP header containing `unsafe-inline` for `script-src` in a Next.js project — flag as a de-prioritized security posture needing a nonce migration.
- Negative: hardcoded IP literal (e.g. `76.76.21.21`) embedded in IaC/Terraform/docs as if it's a stable universal constant.
- Negative: any Drain endpoint config or webhook secret committed as a plaintext value in a repo file rather than referenced from a secret manager/CI secret.
- Negative: evidence of a reverse proxy (Cloudflare proxy/orange-cloud CNAME, custom nginx ingress) sitting in front of a Vercel-hosted domain when Bot Protection or WAF rules are also configured — flag as a signal-degradation risk.
- Negative: no security-header configuration found anywhere in the repo (`vercel.json` and `next.config.js` both absent headers()) for a production Next.js app.

### actionability

**skill_module_bullets** _Bullets that must land in the target references module_:

- Read the apex A-record / CNAME target from your project's own Domains settings each time — do not hardcode `76.76.21.21` or any similar value as a permanent constant.
- Wildcard domains require the Vercel nameservers method (not CNAME/A records) because wildcard cert issuance needs Vercel to control DNS for ACME challenges.
- DDoS mitigation, IP blocking, and WAF custom rules are free on every plan including Hobby; rate limiting and managed rulesets (OWASP CRS, available Enterprise-only) are priced/gated features.
- Never place Cloudflare or another reverse proxy in front of a Vercel deployment that uses Bot Protection — it degrades detection accuracy and causes repeated re-challenges.
- Build Next.js CSP with per-request nonces for inline/hydration scripts instead of `unsafe-inline`; set HSTS, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, and Referrer-Policy via vercel.json or next.config.js headers().
- Drains (the unified successor to "Log Drains") export logs, OTel traces, Speed Insights, Web Analytics, Connect events, and Audit Logs to custom endpoints or native integrations (Datadog, Splunk, Panther, S3, Dash0, Braintrust); general Drains require Pro/Enterprise, Audit Log Drains require Enterprise.
- Instrument with `@vercel/otel`'s registerOTel() rather than a hand-rolled OpenTelemetry SDK — manual setup loses Session Tracing and Trace Drains support; custom spans are unsupported on the Edge runtime.
- Speed Insights measures field Core Web Vitals (RUM); it and Web Analytics do not capture JS exceptions — keep Sentry (or equivalent) as the system of record for application errors alongside these Vercel-native tools.
- Verify Drain payload signatures at your receiving endpoint rather than trusting source IP allowlisting; prefer short-lived/rotatable auth over static API keys for cloud-backed export sinks where supported.
- Vercel does not provide mail service for domains — re-add MX records via a third party (e.g. ImprovMX, Forward Email) after moving DNS to Vercel nameservers, or email silently breaks.
**checklist** _Pre-ship checklist for this surface_:

- Confirm the apex and www domains are both added under Project Settings > Domains with a redirect configured between them.
- Verify DNS records (A/ALIAS/CNAME) match the exact values shown in the live Domains settings, not a cached/hardcoded value.
- Confirm SSL certificate is issued and valid for both apex and www (and any wildcard) domains.
- Enable and review Vercel WAF: at least baseline custom rules on auth/checkout routes, IP blocking for known-bad sources.
- Enable Bot Protection managed ruleset (challenge mode) and make an explicit decision (log/deny) on the AI Bots managed ruleset.
- Confirm no reverse proxy sits in front of the Vercel deployment if Bot Protection/WAF is relied upon.
- Set security headers (CSP with nonces, HSTS, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy) and verify via securityheaders.com.
- Configure at least one Log Drain and one Trace Drain (Pro+) to an external sink before relying on Vercel's own short-retention dashboard views.
- Confirm OTel is wired via @vercel/otel with `registerOTel()` and that traces appear in the target collector/APM.
- Enable Speed Insights and Web Analytics, and set an appropriate sample rate to control cost.
- Confirm Sentry (or equivalent) remains wired for exception/error tracking independent of Vercel Observability.
- Set up Spend Management alerts/caps for Drains volume and Speed Insights overage before go-live.
